It calls `slicc.sigmask_poll` (a 200 ms clock) and `slicc.sigmask_epoll_wait` (a pipe that never gets data, 200 ms) directly, each just after a `SIGALRM` interval timer fired while it computed (it loops on `clock_gettime`, which delivers nothing), once with a wait mask that blocks `SIGALRM` and once with one that doesn't, and prints the result, the events, how often the handler ran and whether the call ended before the timeout.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_29=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 (legacy exceptions) and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/maskentry.wasm` is 40,937 bytes, sha256 `139b24f5b36d7bcdb8f81496dfc0e37f840214aeb4589aca6d3542ac3299a694`.

### `pipetest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/sigpipe.test.mjs` (#810). `pipetest MODE` writes one byte to a pipe whose read end it has closed and prints the result, `errno`, how often its handler ran, and whether `SIGPIPE` is pending. `MODE` is `ignore` (`SIG_IGN`, and the `sigaction` read-back), `handler`, `thread` (a handler, and the write from a second thread, which reports whether the handler ran there), `blocked` (`sigprocmask`), `ignblocked` (`SIG_IGN` and blocked), `dfl` (an explicit `SIG_DFL`), or anything else to leave the disposition as inherited.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_29=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 on `PATH`. `bin/pipetest.wasm` is 57,441 bytes, sha256 `c1c8701cd4599b592d2775b23bd0b1f8e352f1fb189e753067aaa74bb4332a0b`.

### `reaptest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/sigchld-reap.test.mjs` (#820). `reaptest MODE` forks a child that exits with 3 at once and polls `waitpid(WNOHANG)` until the child is no longer running, then reports whether it was waitable or already reaped, then forks two that exit after 100 and 250 ms and reports what `wait` returns and when. `MODE` is `ign` (`SIGCHLD` `SIG_IGN`), `nocldwait` (`SIG_DFL` with `SA_NOCLDWAIT`), `reset` (`SIG_IGN`, then `SIG_DFL`), `dfl`, or `inherited`, which first prints what `slicc.sigaction_inherited` reports for `SIGPIPE`, `SIGCHLD` and `SIGTERM`.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_29=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 (legacy exceptions) and binaryen's `wasm-opt` (`--asyncify` for `proc_fork`) on `PATH`. `bin/reaptest.wasm` is 46,919 bytes, sha256 `b351d53b1a3b317a37642baceb731aef62a1bee387aa8972cba5d9949e1a9a3f`.

### `epolltest`

A C program built with wasixcc against `@ai-ecoverse/wasix-sysroot` 2025.9.30-29, the fixture for the epoll and eventfd rows of `test/integration/epoll.test.mjs` (#674) that tokio can't show:
- level vs edge-triggered, and edge-triggered on ADD and on MOD;
- ONESHOT (silent, hangups included, until MOD);
- the eventfd counter and semaphore;
- EINTR from a SIGALRM handler, and timeouts;
- ctl errors;
- closed descriptors;
- writable edges on a pipe and a TCP socket;
- registrations and eventfd writes from another thread;
- EPOLLRDHUP;
- fairness;
- a nonblocking connect;
- stdin.

`epolltest MODE` runs one of them and prints what it saw.

The program uses the libc's own (Linux) operation numbers, as real C programs do. The same source is built twice: `bin/epolltest.wasm` on -29, whose libc translates them to WASIX's, and `bin/epolltest-28.wasm` (command `epolltest28`) on -28, which passes them untranslated and returns the WASI errno instead of -1 with `errno` set; `ctl()` turns a positive return into `errno`. The ctl errors rows also call the `wasix_32v1.epoll_ctl` import directly with WASIX's numbers (ADD 0, MOD 1, DEL 2), and with 3, which WASIX doesn't define: strict for the -29 build, dual for the -28 one (#684).

The libc does translate the event bits, both ways, and `epoll_create`, `epoll_wait` and `eventfd` work as on Linux.

**Build:**

```sh
WASIX_SYSROOT_29=/path/to/wasix-sysroot-29 WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot-28 ./build.sh
```

- `WASIX_SYSROOT_29` and `WASIX_SYSROOT_28` are the unpacked sysroot packages (sha256 of the -29 tarball `db5e737686da2f720cea32f3e8e681c4715cbacec2a3dd15566b4d605a399927`).
- `wasixcc` (0.4.7, WASIX clang 21.1.2) comes from `PATH`, or is installed by `install-wasixcc.sh` as for `dlfork`.
- The build is reproducible: `bin/epolltest.wasm` is 79,724 bytes, sha256 `a76b37178783e93c246f2523b93e8842c12d60a48a9641d94349080156da0765`; `bin/epolltest-28.wasm` is 79,414 bytes, sha256 `03fc72abe571525f2e512f92794955502ddb226ed14a54fce694c58c65e4187c`.

### `piefork`

A position-independent C program, built like `dlfork` (wasixcc with `WASIXCC_PIC=yes` and legacy exceptions, against `@ai-ecoverse/wasix-sysroot` 2025.9.30-28, then `wasm-opt --asyncify` with `asyncify-imports@wasix_32v1.proc_fork`). It's the fixture of `test/integration/pie-fork.test.mjs` (#708).

What it does:
1. It recurses 300 frames, each holding stack buffers.
2. At the bottom it snapshots the first 64 KiB of memory, from address 16, where the linker puts its data, and forks.
3. Parent and child then compare that memory with the snapshot and scribble 64 KiB of stack.
4. On the way back up, each frame checks its own buffers.

Each side prints `pattern` (a static array's damage), `low` (bytes of low memory changed) and `frames` (damaged frames).

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` (version 132) on `PATH`. `bin/piefork.wasm` is 823,983 bytes, sha256 `0477598c71b02356145d1725548ee0f2b9e53ca0d43c7349f17acc2ff12b59f8`.

### `forktls`

Two builds of one C program (`forktls.c` with `tlsvars.c`), a position-independent one (`bin/forktls-pie.wasm`, `WASIXCC_PIC=yes`) and a static one (`bin/forktls-static.wasm`), plus a side module with a thread-local (`tlsside.c`, `lib/libtlsside.so`), for `test/integration/fork-tls.test.mjs` (#728). Before and after a fork, parent and child call `close` on a descriptor that isn't open and print `errno` (read directly and through a pointer), then bump one thread-local in the `local-exec` TLS model and one in `global-dynamic` (`tls_model` attributes, since wasixcc drops `-ftls-model`), and the side module's, when the program is given its path to `dlopen`. A position-independent program reaches `errno` through an internal global the linker sets with the TLS base, as a `global-dynamic` variable does in either build; a forked child that didn't set it again read `errno` as 0 and those variables at the wrong address.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with homescoop's pinned `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` 133 on `PATH` (the same toolchain rebuilds `bin/piefork.wasm` byte for byte). `bin/forktls-pie.wasm` is 816,066 bytes, sha256 `c5ed6b06036f4d3c42c660fc13ca1a995d647f11021829f2169567574254fd9e`; `bin/forktls-static.wasm` is 45,564 bytes, sha256 `5df019d5f2a4432132c8b7c6f5992d18ecf9d8e4c4be21351acd59c46c3ca48d`; `lib/libtlsside.so` is 736 bytes, sha256 `ecd32e561c051576a99e87caee5e1aca5238c4055e2e551065fa10fe7476d0b5`.

### `threadjmp`

A threaded WASIX C program for `test/integration/thread-asyncify.test.mjs` (#710). A thread longjmps 50 times through a 40-frame recursion and forks. Its child forks again and reports its own child's exit status. Meanwhile the main thread keeps recursing 200 frames deep and checks its frames afterwards (`main damaged`).
- `bin/threadjmp.wasm`: built without exceptions (`WASIXCC_WASM_EXCEPTIONS=no`), so `setjmp` goes through Asyncify's `stack_checkpoint`/`stack_restore`. wasixcc runs Asyncify itself for that build.
- `bin/threadjmp-pie.wasm`: position-independent (`WASIXCC_PIC=yes`, legacy exceptions), with Asyncify for `proc_fork` only. It's built with `-DFORK_ONLY`, because Asyncify can't flatten a function that uses exceptions for `setjmp`.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with homescoop's pinned `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/threadjmp.wasm` is 76,125 bytes, sha256 `351fe15874125b1115f555ea406af9a6b27700bb8a51eb599baf7529ef631b20`; `bin/threadjmp-pie.wasm` is 815,633 bytes, sha256 `9179896d805b005b8d9569286b6e233ee96f718ae3630708c859be3eee5c1c08`.

### `stopwait`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/stop-restart.test.mjs` (#738). `stopwait CALL` forks a child that waits 300 ms in `CALL` (`epoll`, `poll`, `ppoll`, `select`, `pselect`, `nanosleep` or `clock_nanosleep`) on a pipe that never gets data. The parent stops the child once `/proc/<pid>/task/1/stat` shows it blocked (`S`), or 100 ms after it's ready on a kernel without that state, continues it 200 ms later, and reports whether the stop found it blocked. The child prints the result, the error and whether the wait ended before the deadline, at it (300–450 ms) or late.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 (legacy exceptions) and binaryen's `wasm-opt` (`--asyncify` for `proc_fork`) on `PATH`. `bin/stopwait.wasm` is 59,958 bytes, sha256 `ce49e04baed13b7ccf04d8231da4a29567fde0dac19d5cc81f36c185e0eb4555`.

A threaded WASIX C program for `test/integration/thread-signal.test.mjs` (#520). The main thread blocks `SIGUSR1` and `SIGTERM`, and a worker thread unblocks both. Then the program signals itself:

- `sigthread default` sends `SIGTERM` (no handler), which must end the process.

It prints what its own `sigaction(sig, NULL, &old)` reports for `SIGPIPE`, `SIGHUP` and `SIGINT` (`ignored`, `default` or `caught`), then sets `SIGPIPE` back to `SIG_DFL` and reports it again.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_33=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/inherittest.wasm` is 39,068 bytes, sha256 `c561a52eb27b3c1b6204b997f4eec967b9f4869c6ab13e7915a5a94b326a710e`.

### `sigjmptest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/sigjmp.test.mjs` ([#975](https://github.com/ai-ecoverse/slicc-kernel/issues/975), homescoop#1059), built on wasix-sysroot 2025.9.30-36. Each run takes a mode as its only argument:
- `term`: a `SIGTERM` handler with `SIGUSR1` in its `sa_mask` sends itself `SIGUSR1`, which must wait until the handler returns: `term-done usr1`.
- `longjmp`: `longjmp` out of a `SIGUSR1` handler that has `SIGUSR2` in `sa_mask`. It prints the mask after the jump, then unblocks everything and raises `SIGUSR1` again with a handler that returns.
- `lsleep`, `lnanosleep`, `lread`, `lpoll`: the same with a `SIGALRM` that interrupts `sleep`, `nanosleep`, a `read` of an empty pipe, or `poll`. `sleep`, `nanosleep`, `read`, `poll` (no `l`) and `raise` use `sigsetjmp`/`siglongjmp`.
- `direct`, `directmask`: `siglongjmp` with no signal at all. `fork` is `raise` in a forked child.
- `hold` holds `flock` on `sigjmp.lock` for 3 seconds, and creates `sigjmp.held` once it has the lock. `flock` is a `SIGALRM` that interrupts a wait for that lock.

Up to -35, `sigsetjmp` was an ordinary libc function rather than the `setjmp` that LLVM's wasm setjmp/longjmp lowering rewrites, so every `siglongjmp` escaped the program (`[object WebAssembly.Exception]`, exit 70), even `direct` (homescoop#1059). In -36 it is a `setjmp` call site that records the mask, and `siglongjmp` restores it.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_36=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` 133 on `PATH`. Asyncify runs with `asyncify-ignore-indirect`, because functions with wasm `try` blocks can't be flattened. `bin/sigjmptest.wasm` is 52,855 bytes, sha256 `0de328b9cbe3f802dd159db200daa973cc857d3daa4227bca652864b55ebb93f`.

### `handlertest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/handler-defer.test.mjs` (#971), built on wasix-sysroot 2025.9.30-33. Its `SIGTERM` handler counts how often and how deeply it runs. On its first run it sends itself `SIGTERM` again and then writes to stderr. `handlertest` installs it with no flags, and `handlertest nodefer` with `SA_NODEFER`. Either way it prints `the handler ran N times, at most D deep`.

**`nesttest`:** a WASIX C program for `test/integration/handler-defer.test.mjs` (#1236, homescoop#1058), built on wasix-sysroot 2025.9.30-28. Its `SIGUSR1` handler, on its first run, unblocks `SIGUSR1` with `pthread_sigmask` and raises it again. It prints the order of handler entries and exits (`<` and `>`) and the deepest nesting; Linux prints `order=<<>> maxdepth=2`.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_33=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/handlertest.wasm` is 40,458 bytes, sha256 `b0e8e841c053ff133b734de336198410e7705b536e155166fa35970ad1fbb9c2`.

### `fifotest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/fifo.test.mjs` (#896).
- `fifotest snatch PATH N` tries `open(O_RDONLY|O_NONBLOCK)` in a loop until it opens the FIFO or has been refused (`EACCES`) N times, and prints both counts.
- `fifotest mkfifo PATH [MODE [DIR]]` makes a FIFO through its own `slicc_fs.path_mkfifo` declaration (`MODE` in octal, `666` by default), relative to the cwd, or to `DIR` opened as a directory fd, and prints the result.
- `fifotest stat PATH` prints `fifo` or `not a fifo`.
- `fifotest read PATH` opens it for reading (blocking) and prints what it reads until `EOF`. `fifotest nbread PATH` does the same with `O_NONBLOCK`.
- `fifotest write PATH [TEXT]` opens it for writing and writes TEXT. `fifotest nbwrite PATH [TEXT]` does so with `O_NONBLOCK`, and prints `open: No such device or address` with no reader.
- `fifotest alarm PATH [restart]` arms `alarm(1)` with a `SIGALRM` handler (with `SA_RESTART` if asked), then opens the FIFO for reading. It prints `open: Interrupted system call`, or `opened` and what it read. The handler creates the file named by `FIFOTEST_MARK`, so a test can wait for the interrupt instead of a clock.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/fifotest.wasm` is 54,410 bytes, sha256 `bec06cd5cc47e64f1b8871d0c09dd5ca9e679e0239c58825cb94047c34886b40`.

### `fdalias`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/fd-alias.test.mjs` (#894).
- `fdalias PATH...` prints, for each path, what `stat`, `lstat` and `readlink` give: a file type (`fifo`, `chr`, `reg`, `lnk`, `dir`, `sock`) or the error. A `pipe:[N]`/`socket:[N]` link is cut after the `[`, so the inode number doesn't enter the expected output.
- `fdalias cat PATH` opens the path and prints what one read returns.
- `fdalias open PATH` opens the path read-write itself and prints what `readlink("/proc/self/fd/N")` gives for the new descriptor (#909).

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/fdalias.wasm` is 47,699 bytes, sha256 `dad58b44dba8a5edc1dfc8a139a5fdd8105d8aebbdc03609ed144c37dad0f64c`.

### `spintest`

A WASIX C program and a JS command for `test/integration/transport-spin.test.mjs`, which guards the sync transport's paths from #856 (#864).
- `spintest rounds N` does N 1-byte write+read rounds on a pipe, a different byte each round, and prints `N rounds in order` if every byte came back as sent. These are fast replies, which take the transport's spin.
- `spintest wait` prints `waiting` and blocks in a 1-byte `read` of its terminal, a reply that comes only when the test types. The test measures its own process's CPU (all threads) while the read blocks.
- `spintest big M` writes an M MiB file and reads it back in 1 MiB `read` calls, each of which must be whole and match. A 1 MiB read fills the transport window exactly.
- `spinread PATH` (`bin/spinread.mjs`, a JS command) reads a whole file through the synchronous `ctx.sync.fs.readFile` and prints its length and a 31-hash. A file over 1 MiB is a reply larger than the window, so it comes in several chunks.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/spintest.wasm` is 50,095 bytes, sha256 `be7659422f187a97fac412ac1dd2ec9d412e8dcd067da88157960352ded6178b`.

### `siginfotest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/siginfo.test.mjs` (#892). It calls `slicc.sigwait_info` and `slicc.siginfo_take` through its own import declarations, since libc only uses them from wasix-sysroot -33. It prints, for each of these, the signal, `code`, whether `pid` and `uid` are the expected sender's, `status` and the size the kernel filled:
- a forked child's `kill(getppid(), SIGUSR1)` and its exit with 7 (`SIGCHLD`);
- `pthread_kill` to a second thread waiting in `sigwait_info`;
- `kill` of itself;
- a child killed by `SIGTERM`;
- `alarm`;
- a 12-byte struct;
- `siginfo_take` in a handler, and with nothing recorded (`ENOENT`).

`siginfotest setuid UID` instead forks a child that calls `setuid(UID)` and exits with 3, and reports the `SIGCHLD`, whose `uid` must be UID.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/siginfotest.wasm` is 51,578 bytes, sha256 `57f84041bf38d207ecc5aafe0782290ea5e219c414906b7552c108bec8cdc274`.

### `longwait`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/long-timers.test.mjs` (#767).
- `longwait alarm` sets `alarm(3000000)` (about 35 days), sleeps 0.5 s, and prints how many alarms came and how many seconds were left.
- `longwait select|ppoll|nanosleep` waits 3,000,000 s on a pipe that never gets data, until a 300 ms interval timer interrupts the wait, and prints `not before it` when the wait lasted at least 300 ms (`early` otherwise), measured from before `setitimer` arms the timer, so the time the arming call takes can't shorten it (#858). It sets no upper bound, so a loaded CI host can't fail the row; a wait that never ends times the test out.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/longwait.wasm` is 44,002 bytes, sha256 `24acd996068ea04c526b74c93b896986eef782ec96230e19fb939d1b00a1bad0`.

### `epollmask`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/epoll-mask.test.mjs` (#771). It calls `slicc.sigmask_epoll_wait` directly (`import_module("slicc")`) with `SIGUSR1` and `SIGALRM` blocked outside the call, and checks four cases:
- a pending `SIGUSR1` the call's mask unblocks gives `EINTR` at once, with the handler run;
- with nothing ready, a 200 ms wait times out with 0 events;
- a `SIGALRM` from a 100 ms interval timer, unblocked by the call's mask, ends a wait with no timeout;
- with one descriptor ready, the call returns 1 event.

After the calls, a `SIGUSR1` stays pending, which shows the old mask is back.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/epollmask.wasm` is 40,878 bytes, sha256 `63a5482ac4d281800329f7c90615028188d549f733065afc379cce737915f566`.

### `forkrelocs`

A position-independent WASIX C program (`forkrelocs.c`) and a side module (`side.c`, `lib/libforkrelocs.so`), for `test/integration/fork-relocs.test.mjs` (#777). Both have a statically initialized pointer (`int *p = &a`) that the parent changes before it forks (with `dlopen` of the side module when given its path). The child reports whether each pointer kept the parent's value or was reset to its initial one.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with homescoop's pinned `wasixcc` 0.4.7 and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/forkrelocs.wasm` sha256 `7538bc2580d8b4a30912d28a4744d1848544da74d7b47d549a7c80d623a2ed8e`; `lib/libforkrelocs.so` is 753 bytes, sha256 `3dd52124b804670fb22eac46124678d35d8f478da94faf37792406d21e8e9165`.

### `sigwaittest`

A threaded WASIX C program for `test/integration/sigwait.test.mjs` (#520). It calls `slicc.sigwait` directly (`import_module("slicc")`) with `SIGUSR1`, `SIGUSR2` and `SIGTERM` blocked, and checks four cases:
- a pending `SIGUSR1` is taken, and its handler doesn't run;
- with nothing pending, a 200 ms wait gives `EAGAIN`;
- a handled `SIGALRM` (from a 100 ms interval timer) during the wait gives `EINTR`;
- a `SIGALRM` that comes while the program computes, and so runs its handler as `slicc.sigwait` is entered, also gives `EINTR` at once, as it does for the other waits;
- a `SIGALRM` that comes while the program computes, and so runs its handler as `slicc.sigwait` is entered, also gives `EINTR` at once, as it does for the other waits;
- a `SIGTERM` the program sends itself, blocked in every thread, is taken by a second thread that waits for it, and the process runs on.

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 (`-pthread`) and binaryen's `wasm-opt` on `PATH`. `bin/sigwaittest.wasm` is 44,552 bytes, sha256 `51f419544e97d5ec38039212a2940462cec1868947d6de3c33a3533df4646a7b`.
### `semtest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/semaphore.test.mjs` (#717). It calls the `slicc.sem_*` imports directly (`import_module("slicc")`), so the rows don't wait for the libc side. `semtest MODE` runs one of:
- `handoff`, `inherit`, `spawn` (a spawned and an exec'd image open the semaphore by name; exec drops the handles);
- `try`, `timed`, `eintr`, `stopped` (a forked child's 300 ms timed wait is stopped for 200 ms and continued, and reports whether it ended at the deadline; the parent stops it only once `/proc/<pid>/task/1/stat` shows it blocked, `S`, or after 100 ms on a kernel without that state, and reports which);
- `threads` (FIFO, one thread blocked at a time);
- `errors`, `unlink`, `owners setup|user`;
- `killed` (a waiter killed before posts loses no unit), `exited`, `cap [N]` (creates up to N names, 10,000 by default), `handles [N]` (opens up to N, 300 by default).

**Build:** `WASIX_SYSROOT_28=/path/to/wasix-sysroot ./build.sh`, with `wasixcc` 0.4.7 (legacy exceptions, `-pthread`) and binaryen's `wasm-opt` (version 132, `--asyncify` for `proc_fork`) on `PATH`. `bin/semtest.wasm` is 69,366 bytes, sha256 `b3202708f57acad5f51e5be1b06e3b5a0ad02dd84b3791e052787117b828da4e`.

### `wlocktest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/wasix-locks.test.mjs` (#722). It calls `slicc.fcntl_lock` directly (`import_module("slicc")`), so the rows don't wait for the wasix-sysroot side. Its arguments are a sequence of steps, run in order:
- `open SLOT r|w|rw PATH`, `seek SLOT OFFSET`, `close SLOT`;
- `set|setw|get SLOT TYPE WHENCE START LEN`, which call `F_SETLK`, `F_SETLKW` or `F_GETLK` on a struct pre-filled with `0x5a`. `get` reports the holder, or whether every byte but `l_type` stayed as it was;
- `cmd SLOT N`, `badfd`;
- `touch PATH`, `await PATH`, `sleep MS`, `pid`, `trap` (a `SIGUSR1` handler), `fork SLOT` (the child runs `F_GETLK` and `F_SETLK` on the slot).

**Build:** `INSTALL_WASIXCC=<homescoop>/scripts/install-wasixcc.sh WASIX_SYSROOT_28=<unpacked @ai-ecoverse/wasix-sysroot 2025.9.30-28> ./build.sh`, with homescoop's pinned wasixcc 0.4.7 (LLVM 21.1.206, binaryen 133) and Homebrew's binaryen `wasm-opt` 132 on `PATH` for the Asyncify pass (`proc_fork`). Two builds give the same bytes: `bin/wlocktest.wasm` is 56,958 bytes, sha256 `31308a54e13e7dcac497ad9d8a05799008c8beaa4ae3a36c76e218af7235ec23`.

### `flocktest`

A WASIX C program for `test/integration/flock.test.mjs` (#893). It calls `slicc.flock` and `slicc.fcntl_lock` directly (`import_module("slicc")`). Its arguments are a sequence of steps, run in order:
- `open SLOT r|w|rw PATH`, `pipe SLOT SLOT`, `pair SLOT SLOT` (a `socketpair`), `dup FROM TO`, `fd SLOT N` (names an inherited descriptor), `close SLOT`;
- `flock SLOT OP`, where OP is made of `sh`, `ex`, `un` and `nb` (`exnb` is `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`) or is a number; `badfd`; `fcntl SLOT r|w|u` (`F_SETLK` on the whole file), `fcntlw SLOT r|w` (`F_SETLKW`);
- `fork … end` (the child runs the steps up to the matching `end`, its lines start with `child `, and the parent waits for it before it goes on after `end`);
- `touch PATH`, `await PATH`, `sleep MS`, `say TEXT`, `trap` (a `SIGUSR1` handler), `trapr` (one with `SA_RESTART` that prints `handler`).

**Build:** as `wlocktest`. Two builds give the same bytes: `bin/flocktest.wasm` is 58,907 bytes, sha256 `58b16f19911e0665ba1fbda3fa6d370d6228f4c59dfc13902f126c6e8b43b120`.
